And... no, no one has said anything about me talking too fast or anything like that. It's really just been the freezing up and getting confused during my shifts that people noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I used to do a lot of different things, but I don't really go for beach walks, bake, garden, or write in my journal much anymore. \n\nAnd it's affecting things at home, too. Like, when my husband tries to talk with me, I mostly just sit there. It's not that I'm trying to ignore him or anything, it just feels like... I don't know, like I just can't get myself to care or even start anything at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the loss of interest and difficulty engaging in activities?\n\nPatient: I guess this whole change in me has been building for a while now... probably about a year and two months. It seemed to start right around the time I was asked to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed or sad. It's not like a heavy, hopeless feeling... it's just more like I'm empty or blank. I just don't have the energy or desire to do anything, but I don't feel down in the dumps about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements have slowed down, or that you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't feel restless or jittery, and... well, I don't think I'm moving any slower than normal. I mean, I sit around a lot more now just because I don't have the drive to get up and do things, but my actual physical movements seem regular to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to find enjoyable, like baking or writing in your journal, no longer give you any satisfaction or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's pretty much it. Or I'd start doing things completely out of order. \n\nLike, I'd end up just standing over by the soda station holding the cash drawer key, and I'd be repeating table numbers out loud instead of actually closing out the checks. It really freaked my coworkers out, so... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energized, needed less sleep, or found yourself talking more rapidly than usual, even if others told you you were acting out of the ordinary?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I haven't really had anything like that happen. I definitely haven't felt super energized or felt like I didn't need to sleep. And... no, no one has said anything about me talking too fast or anything like that. It's really just been the freezing up and getting confused during my shifts that people noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed a loss of interest or pleasure in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies, work, or spending time with friends and family?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I used to do a lot of different things, but I don't really go for beach walks, bake, garden, or write in my journal much anymore. \n\nAnd it's affecting things at home, too. Like, when my husband tries to talk with me, I mostly just sit there. It's not that I'm trying to ignore him or anything, it just feels like... I don't know, like I just can't get myself to care or even start anything at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the loss of interest and difficulty engaging in activities?\n\nPatient: I guess this whole change in me has been building for a while now... probably about a year and two months. It seemed to start right around the time I was asked to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed or sad. It's not like a heavy, hopeless feeling... it's just more like I'm empty or blank. I just don't have the energy or desire to do anything, but I don't feel down in the dumps about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements have slowed down, or that you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't feel restless or jittery, and... well, I don't think I'm moving any slower than normal. I mean, I sit around a lot more now just because I don't have the drive to get up and do things, but my actual physical movements seem regular to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to find enjoyable, like baking or writing in your journal, no longer give you any satisfaction or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's pretty much it. I don't really bake or write in my journal much anymore. I've stopped gardening and going for beach walks, too. I just don't get anything out of them now. It even affects things at home... like, when my husband tries to talk with me, I mostly just sit there. It just feels like I can't get myself to care or start anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a lot of fatigue or loss of energy, even when you haven't been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm not physically tired or exhausted. It's more just... like I was saying, not having the motivation to start things. But my actual energy feels fine, I think. I just don't really use it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re doing much less than you used to, even in basic daily tasks like getting dressed, cooking, or cleaning around the house?\n\nPatient: No, not exactly. I mean, I still get dressed and shower, and I can make a sandwich or something if I'm hungry. I'm not... I haven't stopped doing the basics. It's more just the hobbies and talking to people that I don't do. But I still manage to keep myself and the house somewhat put together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your lack of motivation or difficulty starting activities has been happening almost every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I haven't really kept track of it like that. I mean... I don't think it's been every single day for two weeks straight. It just feels like it happens a lot, but I couldn't say for sure if it's been that continuous without a break.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if the lack of motivation or difficulty starting activities has occurred on most days, for most of the time, over the past year or so?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I think you just asked me that. But no, like I said, I really can't think of any time where it lasted two whole weeks without a break. I always end up having a day in there where I can get up and, you know, go to my shifts at the restaurant or just do things around the house. So no, never two weeks straight.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, can you identify a specific time when you felt this way—like you couldn’t get yourself to do things—for at least two weeks in a row, without much of a break?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're going in circles here. I always have a day or two where I get back on my feet and go to work or take care of things. It just hasn't been two weeks straight like you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there has ever been a time when you felt low, empty, or sad most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... Just a day here and there, like anyone else, I guess. Nothing that stretches out like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt detached from yourself, like you were watching yourself from outside your body, or felt disconnected from the world around you?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. It seemed to start right around the time I was asked to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities at work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, depressed, or hopeless most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel depressed or sad. It's not like a heavy, hopeless feeling... it's just more like I'm empty or blank. I just don't have the energy or desire to do anything, but I don't feel down in the dumps about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your movements have slowed down, or that you feel restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed anything like that. I don't feel restless or jittery, and... well, I don't think I'm moving any slower than normal. I mean, I sit around a lot more now just because I don't have the drive to get up and do things, but my actual physical movements seem regular to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that things you used to find enjoyable, like baking or writing in your journal, no longer give you any satisfaction or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's pretty much it. I don't really bake or write in my journal much anymore. I've stopped gardening and going for beach walks, too. I just don't get anything out of them now. It even affects things at home... like, when my husband tries to talk with me, I mostly just sit there. It just feels like I can't get myself to care or start anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing a lot of fatigue or loss of energy, even when you haven't been doing much physically?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I'm not physically tired or exhausted. It's more just... like I was saying, not having the motivation to start things. But my actual energy feels fine, I think. I just don't really use it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’re doing much less than you used to, even in basic daily tasks like getting dressed, cooking, or cleaning around the house?\n\nPatient: No, not exactly. I mean, I still get dressed and shower, and I can make a sandwich or something if I'm hungry. I'm not... I haven't stopped doing the basics. It's more just the hobbies and talking to people that I don't do. But I still manage to keep myself and the house somewhat put together.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your lack of motivation or difficulty starting activities has been happening almost every day for at least two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I haven't really kept track of it like that. I mean... I don't think it's been every single day for two weeks straight. It just feels like it happens a lot, but I couldn't say for sure if it's been that continuous without a break.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if the lack of motivation or difficulty starting activities has occurred on most days, for most of the time, over the past year or so?\n\nPatient: I don't think so. I mean, it hasn't been going on for a whole year. It's more recent than that. I haven't been feeling this way for that long, just... lately, I guess. But definitely not most days for a year.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there was a period of at least two weeks where you felt this way most of the time, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Like I was saying before, I really don't think it's been a solid two weeks. So, no, I don't think there was a two-week period exactly like what you're asking.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there was ever a time when you felt this way—like you couldn’t get yourself to do things—for at least two weeks straight, without much of a break?\n\nPatient: I... I always have a day or two where I get back on my feet and go to work or take care of things. It just hasn't been two weeks straight like you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if there has ever been a time when you felt low, empty, or sad most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... So, no, not for two solid weeks.\n\nDoctor: Over the past two years, have you had periods where you felt this lack of motivation or emptiness more days than not, even if not every single day?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Just a day here and there, like anyone else, I guess. I'm always just... right here in my own body.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced times where things around you felt unreal, like the world seemed foggy or dreamlike, even if you weren't high or sleep-deprived?\n\nPatient: No, nothing like that.
